<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Bogomolov–Pop reconstruction conjecture for function fields of transcendence degree at least two over arbitrary algebraically closed fields of characteristic different from <i>ℓ</i>. The pro-<i>ℓ</i> abelian-by-central datum determines the perfect closure and its constant field, with precisely the Frobenius and <i>ℓ</i>-adic unit ambiguities.

- **claim-perfect-closure-reconstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $k$ and $l$ be arbitrary algebraically closed fields of characteristics different from $\ell$, and let $K/k$ and $L/l$ be function fields with $\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(K/k),\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(L/l)\ge2$. Then $$\Phi_{K,L}:\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}_F^i(K,L)\longrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}^c(\Pi_L^a,\Pi_K^a)/\mathbb Z_\ell^\times, \qquad [\alpha]\longmapsto[\alpha^*],$$ is a bijection.
  - Explanation: The abelian-by-central datum determines the perfect closure together with its constant field. The field side is taken modulo Frobenius in positive characteristic, and the Galois side is taken modulo a global unit. Characteristics and relative transcendence degrees need not be identified beforehand.
- **claim-continuous-evaluation-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-duality)): The group $F^\times/\kappa^\times$ is free abelian. Consequently $W_F$ is a product of copies of $\mathbb Z_\ell$, and evaluation gives $$\widehat F=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{cont}}(W_F,\mathbb Z_\ell),\qquad \widehat F/\ell^e\widehat F=F^\times/F^{\times\ell^e}.$$ The module $\widehat F$ is torsion free, and evaluations on $W_F$ detect every finite-level class.
  - Explanation: Divisor evaluation makes the multiplicative group modulo constants free abelian. Its completion is the continuous evaluation dual of the character group, and finite-level classes are detected by characters.
- **claim-subfield-completion-and-norm** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-completion)): Let $E\subset F$ be function fields over the same algebraically closed field $\kappa$.* 1. *If $E$ is relatively algebraically closed in $F$, then $\widehat E\to\widehat F$ is injective, its image is closed and $\ell$-saturated, and restriction $W_F\to W_E$ is a continuous surjection. Here $\ell$-saturated means that $\ell h\in\widehat E$ for $h\in\widehat F$ implies $h\in\widehat E$.* 2. *If $F/E$ is finite, its completion map is injective, and inclusion followed by norm on completions is multiplication by $[F:E]$.
  - Explanation: Relatively algebraically closed subfields embed as closed saturated completed subgroups and admit surjective character restriction. Finite extensions also give injective completion maps, with the norm composite recording the extension degree.
- **claim-bracket-compatible-central-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-central-lift)): Every bracket-compatible isomorphism in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is the abelianization of a continuous isomorphism $\Pi_L^c\to\Pi_K^c$. This holds also for $\ell=2$.
  - Explanation: A bracket-compatible abelian isomorphism admits a continuous lift to the central quotient. Existence follows from a splitting of the associated abelian extension; the statement does not assert uniqueness of the lift.
- **claim-point-order-kernels-and-genus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-curve-kernel)): If $\operatorname{char}\kappa\ne\ell$, then $$\ker\left(E^\times/E^{\times\ell} \xrightarrow{(\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_P)_P}\bigoplus_{P\in C}\mathbb Z/\ell\right) \simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(C)[\ell].$$ Its dimension over $\mathbb F_\ell$ is $2g(C)$. The kernel of all exact point orders on $\widehat E$ is the Tate module $T_\ell\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(C)$ and has $\mathbb Z_\ell$-rank $2g(C)$.
  - Explanation: Point orders leave a kernel accounted for by Picard torsion at finite level and by the Picard Tate module on the completion. The size of these kernels recovers the genus when the characteristic restriction holds.
- **claim-commutator-alternating-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-commuting)): Let $F/\kappa$ be a function field over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $\ell$. Under Kummer duality $W_F=\Pi_F^a$, two elements of $W_F$ are alternating if and only if their commutator in $\Delta_F$ is zero. This assertion holds also for $\ell=2$.
  - Explanation: The central commutator vanishes exactly when the corresponding characters satisfy the alternating relation. The proof treats the prime with no division by the characteristic-sensitive denominator.
- **claim-residual-character-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-residue)): For a quasi-prime $r$-divisor $v$ of $F/\kappa$, restriction to units induces a canonical topological isomorphism $$D_v/I_v\cong W_{Fv}.$$ If $f,g\in\mathcal D_v$, they are alternating if and only if their residual characters in $\mathcal G_{Fv}$ are alternating.
  - Explanation: Restriction to units identifies decomposition modulo inertia with the residue character group. Alternating relations can therefore be transported through valuations without invoking residue-field Galois groups.
- **claim-alternating-dimension-recovers-transcendence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-dimension)): For $F/\kappa$ as above, in any characteristic, $s$ is the maximum dimension of an alternating subspace of $\mathcal G_F$. If $S$ has dimension $s$, its valuative subspace is $\mathcal I_u$ for its associated valuation $u$, and $u$ attains equality in <a href="#local:abhyankar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="local:abhyankar">[local:abhyankar]</a>.
  - Explanation: The maximum alternating dimension equals relative transcendence degree. At maximum dimension the valuative part is a full inertia space, and its valuation has no transcendence defect.
- **claim-quasiprime-inertia-recognition** (classification, [source](#stmt-local-quasiprime)): Assume $s\geq2$. A line $H\subset\mathcal G_F$ is the inertia space of a quasi-prime divisor if and only if there are two $s$-dimensional alternating subspaces $S_1,S_2$ with $S_1\cap S_2=H$. The valuation is unique. If $0\ne h\in H$, its decomposition space is $$\mathcal D_v=\{f\in\mathcal G_F:(h,f)\text{ is alternating}\}.$$
  - Explanation: An inertia line is recognized by its expression as the intersection of maximum-dimensional alternating spaces. The line determines its valuation uniquely and recovers decomposition through the alternating relation.
- **claim-quasiprime-flag-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-transport)): Let $K/k$ and $L/l$ be function fields over algebraically closed fields of characteristic different from $\ell$, each of transcendence degree at least two. Let $\varphi:W_L\to W_K$ be a continuous bracket-compatible $\mathbb Z_\ell$-module isomorphism. Then their transcendence degrees agree, say they are $d$. For every $1\leq r<d$, the map $\varphi$ carries exactly the pairs $I_w\subset D_w$ of quasi-prime $r$-divisors of $L/l$ onto the corresponding pairs of $K/k$. For matched pairs it induces a continuous isomorphism $$D_w/I_w\cong W_{Lw}\ \longrightarrow\ D_v/I_v\cong W_{Kv}$$ that preserves alternating pairs.
  - Explanation: Bracket compatibility recovers the common dimension, the exact integral inertia and decomposition pairs, and their residual character isomorphisms. Iteration recognizes the successive quasi-prime flags.
- **claim-residual-criterion-preconditions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-criterion-hypotheses)): Every quasi-prime $(s-1)$-divisor $v$ of $F/\kappa$ satisfies the two hypotheses of the residual-curve criterion.
  - Explanation: Quasi-prime flags satisfy the value-group obstruction and the auxiliary-subfield condition required by the residual-curve criterion. This permits that criterion to be applied to the intrinsically recovered local data.
- **claim-finite-constant-residual-curve-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-curves)): In the setting of Proposition <a href="#local:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="local:transport">10</a>, let $w$ and $v$ be matched quasi-prime $(d-1)$-divisors. Then $lw$ is algebraic over a finite field if and only if $kv$ is. When this holds, the induced isomorphism $W_{Lw}\to W_{Kv}$ carries the full family of point inertia subgroups of the respective smooth projective curves onto each other. Corresponding normalized order characters differ by units of $\mathbb Z_\ell$.
  - Explanation: The residual-curve criterion identifies finite-field-closure constants simultaneously on both sides and transports all point inertia subgroups. The associated generators can differ by units, so exact order values are not asserted to be equal.
- **claim-finite-residual-tests** (construction, [source](#stmt-tests-residual)): There is an integer $G_X\ge0$, depending only on the embedded variety $X$, with the following property. Given $g_1,\ldots,g_r\in L^\times$ and $q=\ell^e$, $e\ge1$, there is a quasi-prime $(d-1)$-divisor $w$ of $L/l$ such that:* 1. *$lw$ is an algebraic closure of a finite field of characteristic different from $\ell$, and $Lw=lw(C)$ for a smooth projective integral curve $C/lw$ with $g(C)\le G_X$;* 2. *$w(g_j)=0$ for every $j$, and, for every $(n_1,\ldots,n_r)\in(\mathbb Z/q)^r$, $$\prod_jg_j^{n_j}\in L^{\times q} \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \prod_j\overline g_j^{n_j}\in(Lw)^{\times q};$$* 3. *for $m=\ell$ and $m=q$, $$\label{tests:support} \#\{P\in C(lw):\operatorname{ord}_P(\overline g_j) \not\equiv0\pmod m\}=s_m(g_j) \qquad(1\le j\le r).$$* *Here $\overline g_j$ is the residue of $g_j$ at $w$.
  - Explanation: A prescribed finite collection of multiplicative classes can be tested on a residual curve while preserving its full finite-level relation kernel and the weighted support counts. The test curve may change with the collection and exponent, but the genus bound depends only on the fixed embedded model.
- **claim-quasifinite-bertini-sections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tests-bertini)): Let $V$ be an integral variety over an algebraically closed field, and let $f:V\to\mathbb P^N$ be quasi-finite, with $\dim V=a\ge2$. For a general hyperplane $H$, the inverse image $f^{-1}(H)$ is geometrically irreducible. If $f$ factors as a finite étale map to a smooth locally closed subvariety $U\subset\mathbb P^N$, this inverse image is also smooth for a general $H$.
  - Explanation: General hyperplane sections preserve geometric irreducibility for the stated quasi-finite maps. Smoothness requires the additional factorization through a finite étale map to a smooth embedded open variety.
- **claim-uniform-pencil-support** (bound, [source](#stmt-pencil-uniform)): For every $t\in K\setminus k$, there is a finite constant $B_t$ such that $$\sum_{D\in\operatorname{Supp}_X(\Theta[t-a])}\deg D\le B_t \qquad\text{for every }a\in k.$$ In particular, all these supports are finite, with a bound independent of the member of the pencil.
  - Explanation: The total weighted divisor support is uniformly bounded across an entire pencil. The proof tests sufficiently high finite levels and uses fixed anchor fibers to control the separable residual map before passing to exact supports.
- **claim-individual-finite-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pencil-finite)): For every $z\in K^\times$, the class $\Theta[z]$ has finite support on $X$.
  - Explanation: Every ordinary multiplicative class has finite divisor support after applying the completed Kummer isomorphism. Constant classes vanish, and nonconstant classes occur in a pencil covered by the uniform theorem.
- **claim-completed-curve-subfield-bijection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-recovery-curves)): The map $\Theta$ gives a bijection between the completed curve subfields of $K/k$ and those of $L/l$. More precisely, for every curve subfield $E\subset K$ there is a unique curve subfield $P\subset L$ such that $$\Theta(\widehat E)=\widehat P.$$
  - Explanation: Completed multiplicative groups of relatively algebraically closed curve subfields are preserved bijectively. Incidence gives initial containment over a finite extension; norms, saturation, and finite-rank intersections descend it and force equality and uniqueness.
- **claim-infinite-pencil-divisors-and-residue-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-recovery-residue-powers)): The set $\mathcal S$ is infinite. For every $z\in E^\times$, every $j\geq1$, and every representative $g\in L^\times$ of $\Theta[z]\bmod\ell^j$, all but finitely many $D\in\mathcal S$ satisfy $$\label{eq:recovery-residue-powers} \operatorname{ord}_D(g)=0, \qquad g\|_D\in l(D)^{\times\ell^j}.$$
  - Explanation: The pencil detects infinitely many divisors. Each individual finite-level representative from the completed curve field restricts to a power on all but finitely many of them; the exceptional set may depend on the representative and exponent.
- **claim-common-incidence-curve** (construction, [source](#stmt-recovery-incidence)): Let $l$ be algebraically closed, let $X/l$ be a normal integral projective variety of dimension $d\geq2$, and let $\mathcal S$ be an infinite set of prime divisors of bounded degree in a fixed projective embedding. Let $\mathcal A$ be any collection of pairs $(g,q)$, where $g\in l(X)^\times$ and $q\geq1$ is prime to $\operatorname{char}l$. Suppose that, for each pair separately, all but finitely many $D\in\mathcal S$ satisfy $$\operatorname{ord}_D(g)=0, \qquad g\|_D\in l(D)^{\times q}.$$ There exist a finite field extension $M/l(X)$ and a curve field $P_0/l$ contained in $M$ such that $M/P_0$ is regular and $$g\in(M\overline{P_0})^{\times q} \quad\text{for every }(g,q)\in\mathcal A.$$ The same fields $M$ and $P_0$ work for the entire collection.
  - Explanation: An infinite bounded-degree divisor family produces a fixed incidence curve and a finite extension that geometrically split every test. The parameter curve is fixed before testing; test-dependent normal covers extend each generic splitting to that same curve.
- **claim-regular-kummer-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-recovery-kummer-descent)): Let $M/P$ be a regular field extension, let $q$ be prime to the characteristic, and suppose $\mu_q\subset P$. If $g\in M^\times$ becomes a $q$-th power in $M\overline P$, then its class in $M^\times/M^{\times q}$ comes from a unique class in $P^\times/P^{\times q}$.
  - Explanation: Geometric power splitting over a regular extension descends to a unique base-field Kummer class. Hilbert’s theorem identifies the descended class, and relative algebraic closedness ensures uniqueness.
- **claim-distinct-curve-intersection-finite-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-recovery-intersection)): Let $N/l$ be a function field over algebraically closed constants of characteristic different from $\ell$. If $P$ and $Q$ are distinct curve subfields of $N/l$, then $\widehat P\cap\widehat Q\subset\widehat N$ has finite $\mathbb Z_\ell$-rank.
  - Explanation: An element lying in both completed curve fields has every point order zero on either curve. It therefore lies in a finite-rank Picard Tate module, so an infinite-rank common subgroup forces the curve fields to coincide.
- **claim-true-divisorial-recognition** (classification, [source](#stmt-recovery-divisorial)): A quasi-prime divisor $v$ of $K/k$ is divisorial if and only if $$\label{eq:recovery-divisorial} D_v^\perp\cap\widehat E=0$$ for some curve subfield $E$ of $K/k$. Consequently $\varphi$ preserves divisorial inertia and decomposition groups in both directions.
  - Explanation: True divisorial valuations are distinguished from valuations nontrivial on constants by the existence of a curve completion on which decomposition has trivial annihilator. The completed curve-field correspondence makes this distinction intrinsic and transportable.
- **claim-point-inertia-and-rational-quotients** (classification, [source](#stmt-recovery-rational)): The point inertia lines of every curve subfield $E$ are precisely the saturations of the nonzero images $\rho_E(I_v)$ for divisorial valuations $v$ of $K/k$. The correspondence of Theorem <a href="#recovery:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="recovery:curves">17</a> preserves these lines and the genus. In particular, it matches exactly the relatively algebraically closed rational subfields.* *If $\Theta(\widehat E)=\widehat P$, the induced continuous isomorphism $\varphi_E:W_P\to W_E$ makes the diagram $$\label{eq:recovery-quotient-diagram} \begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large] W_L \arrow[r,"\varphi"] \arrow[d,two heads,"\rho_P"'] & W_K \arrow[d,two heads,"\rho_E"] \\ W_P \arrow[r,"\varphi_E"'] & W_E \end{tikzcd}$$ commute and matches the point inertia lines on its targets. For rational $E$ and $P$, these are the geometric rational quotient diagrams, with their point decomposition data.
  - Explanation: Saturating nonzero divisorial inertia images recovers curve point inertia even when restriction has nontrivial index. These point families recover genus and identify rational subfields. The induced quotient isomorphisms commute with the fixed top isomorphism and retain point decomposition data.
- **claim-pop-graph-and-field-reconstruction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-reconstruction-pop)): Let $F/\kappa$ and $F'/\kappa'$ be function fields of transcendence degree greater than one over algebraically closed fields of characteristic different from $\ell$.* 1. *From $\Pi_F^c\to\Pi_F^a$ and the union of its true divisorial inertia groups, one recovers the total decomposition graph, functorially under class-two isomorphisms whose abelianizations preserve this union.* 2. *Suppose an isomorphism between the total decomposition graphs of $F$ and $F'$ is compatible with all their geometric rational quotient diagrams. Then its map on the top abelian groups is induced, up to one unit in $\mathbb Z_\ell$, by an isomorphism of perfect closures in the opposite direction carrying the constant fields onto each other. That field isomorphism is unique modulo Frobenius twists in positive characteristic, and unique in characteristic zero.* *In (2), a geometric rational quotient is restriction to $\kappa(u)\subset F$ with $F/\kappa(u)$ regular, together with the point inertia and decomposition data on its target. Compatibility means that restriction commutes with the graph isomorphisms and the induced isomorphisms of these targets.
  - Explanation: The cited reconstruction input has separate graph and field stages. True divisorial inertia recovers the graph; compatibility with geometric rational quotient diagrams then recovers the perfect-closure isomorphism and its precise ambiguity.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Function field over algebraically closed constants** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A function field $F/\kappa$ in this paper is a finitely generated extension of an algebraically closed field $\kappa$.
- **Abelian and abelian-by-central pro-ell quotients** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $\operatorname{char}F\ne\ell$, let $G_F^{(\ell)}$ be the maximal pro-$\ell$ quotient of the absolute Galois group, and set $$\Pi_F^a=G_F^{(\ell)}/[G_F^{(\ell)},G_F^{(\ell)}],\qquad \Pi_F^c=G_F^{(\ell)}/[[G_F^{(\ell)},G_F^{(\ell)}],G_F^{(\ell)}].$$
- **Central kernel** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The kernel $\Delta_F=\ker(\Pi_F^c\to\Pi_F^a)$ is central.
- **Bracket-compatible isomorphism** ([source](#pm-h-3)): An isomorphism $\varphi:\Pi_L^a\to\Pi_K^a$ is *bracket-compatible* if it is a continuous $\mathbb Z_\ell$-module isomorphism and there is a continuous $\mathbb Z_\ell$-module isomorphism $\psi:\Delta_L\to\Delta_K$ such that $$\psi([\sigma,\tau]_L)=[\varphi\sigma,\varphi\tau]_K \quad(\sigma,\tau\in\Pi_L^a).$$
- **Perfect closure** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $F^i$ for the perfect closure of $F$; in characteristic zero this is $F$ itself.
- **Frobenius orbit of field isomorphisms** ([source](#pm-h-3)): If the common characteristic is $p>0$, put $$\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}_F^i(K,L)=\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}^i(K,L)/ \bigl(\alpha\sim\mathop{\mathrm{Frob}}_L^n\circ\alpha,\ n\in\mathbb Z\bigr).$$
- **Character group and completed multiplicative group** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For a function field $F/\kappa$ over algebraically closed constants, put $$W_F=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(F^\times,\mathbb Z_\ell),\qquad \widehat F=\varprojlim_{e\ge1} F^\times/F^{\times\ell^e}.$$
- **Evaluation-dual isomorphism** ([source](#eq:theta)): An isomorphism $\varphi:\Pi_L^a\to\Pi_K^a$ has evaluation dual $$\label{eq:theta} \Theta:\widehat K\longrightarrow\widehat L, \qquad \Theta(h)(\sigma)=h(\varphi\sigma).$$
- **Alternating character pair** ([source](#local:alternating)): A pair $f,g\in\mathcal G_F$ is *alternating* if $$\label{local:alternating} f(x)g(1-x)=f(1-x)g(x)\qquad(x\in F\setminus\{0,1\}).$$
- **Minimized inertia and decomposition groups** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Set $$U_v=\mathcal O_v^\times,\qquad U_v^1=1+\mathfrak m_v, \qquad I_v=U_v^\perp\subset D_v=(U_v^1)^\perp\subset W_F,$$ and write $\mathcal I_v=I_v\otimes\mathbb Q_\ell$ and $\mathcal D_v=D_v\otimes\mathbb Q_\ell$.
- **Valuative subset** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A subset of $\mathcal G_F$ is *valuative* if it is contained in $\mathcal I_v$ for some valuation $v$.
- **Quasi-prime divisor** ([source](#local:rank)): A *quasi-prime divisor* is a valuation minimal among those satisfying $$vF/v\kappa\cong\mathbb Z, \qquad \operatorname{trdeg}(Fv/\kappa v)=s-1.$$
- **Quasi-prime divisor flag** ([source](#local:rank)): A quasi-prime $r$-divisor is a composition of $r$ successive quasi-prime divisors of the successive residue function fields.
- **Complete-curve-like inertia family** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A compact abelian pro-$\ell$ group $G$ with a family $(T_i)$ of procyclic subgroups is *complete-curve-like* if one can choose generators $\tau_i$ so that $\tau_i$ tends to zero outside finite subsets, the resulting map $$\prod_i\mathbb Z_\ell\longrightarrow G, \qquad(a_i)\longmapsto\sum_i a_i\tau_i$$ has kernel the diagonal copy of $\mathbb Z_\ell$, and its cokernel is a finitely generated $\mathbb Z_\ell$-module.
- **Finite-level and exact divisor support** ([source](#sec:pencil)): For $h\in\widehat L$ and $e\ge1$, define $$S_e(h)=\{D\subset X\text{ prime divisor}: \operatorname{ord}_D(h)\not\equiv0\pmod{\ell^e}\}, \qquad \operatorname{Supp}_X(h)=\{D:\operatorname{ord}_D(h)\ne0\}.$$
- **Curve subfield** ([source](#sec:curve-recovery)): A *curve subfield* of $K/k$ will mean a subfield $E\subset K$ containing $k$, relatively algebraically closed in $K$, and of transcendence degree one over $k$.
- **Divisorial valuation** ([source](#pm-h-18)): Throughout this subsection, a *divisorial valuation* means such a quasi-prime divisor, with its discrete value group normalized to $\mathbb Z$.
- **Total decomposition graph** ([source](#sec:reconstruction)): The *total decomposition graph* records, at each such flag, the abelian pro-$\ell$ group of the residue field, and at each next divisorial valuation its inertia and decomposition subgroups and the quotient map to the next residue group.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-reconstruction-transcendence-threshold | Relative transcendence degrees of the input function fields | ≥ | $2$ | Both input function fields independently satisfy the threshold. |
| q-point-order-kernel-dimension | Dimension of the finite-level point-order kernel over the residue coefficient field | = | $2g(C)$ | The genus is that of the smooth projective curve. |
| q-exact-order-kernel-rank | Rank of the exact point-order kernel on the completed curve field | = | $2g(C)$ | The completed kernel uses all point orders. |
| q-maximum-alternating-dimension | Maximum dimension of an alternating subspace | = | $s$ | The source parameter is the relative transcendence degree. |
| q-residual-test-genus-budget | Genus of the residual test curve | ≤ | $G_X$ | The genus budget depends only on the embedded model; the residual curve may depend on the finite collection and exponent. |
| q-pencil-hurwitz-coefficient | c_\ell | = | $3-5/\ell$ | The coefficient depends on the fixed prime. |
| q-pencil-positive-coefficient-floor | c_\ell | ≥ | $1/2$ | The coefficient is positive for every allowed prime. |
| q-residual-morphism-degree-budget | B | = | $\max\left\{1,\frac{2G_X-2+\sum_{i=1}^5N_i}{c_\ell}\right\}$ | The constant is independent of the additional pencil member and the sufficiently high testing exponent. |
| q-separable-residual-map-degree | n | ≤ | $B$ | The estimate concerns the separable part after removing inseparability. |
| q-finite-level-pencil-support-budget | \sum_{D\in S_e(h_a)}\deg D | ≤ | $2B$ | The bound is independent of the member, testing level, finite descent data, and residual test; the lower threshold on the exponent can depend on the member. |
| q-exact-pencil-support-budget | Weighted degree of the exact divisor support of a pencil member | ≤ | $B_t$ | The constant is uniform in the pencil member, with model, isomorphism, parameter, and anchors fixed. |
| q-explicit-pencil-budget-multiplier | B_t | = | $2B$ | This is the chosen proof constant, with the residual morphism budget fixed by genus and anchor supports. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-evaluation-duality** [claim](#stmt-prelim-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-duality): Embed multiplicative classes modulo constants into the free divisor group, then describe continuous evaluation on the resulting character product. -> Torsion-free completed multiplicative group and finite-level evaluation duality.
- **step-completion-inclusions** [claim](#stmt-prelim-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-completion): Split the free multiplicative quotient for relatively algebraically closed inclusions and use the norm composite for finite extensions. -> Closed saturated completed subfields, surjective character restriction, and injectivity across finite extensions.
- **step-central-lifting** [claim](#stmt-prelim-central-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-central-lift): Take the fiber product of central extensions, quotient by the diagonal kernel, and split the resulting abelian extension by projectivity. -> A continuous lift to the abelian-by-central quotient.
- **step-curve-order-kernels** [claim](#stmt-prelim-curve-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-curve-kernel): Associate a divisible principal divisor to a torsion Picard class and take the inverse limit of the finite-level identifications. -> Finite-level Picard kernel and exact-order Tate-module kernel recovering genus.
- **step-commutator-recognition** [claim](#stmt-local-commuting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-commuting): Identify the inflation kernel through Steinberg cup relations and transgression, then separate central elements by finite characters. -> Equivalence of vanishing commutators and alternating character pairs.
- **step-residual-character-spaces** [claim](#stmt-local-residue) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-residue): Dualize the exact unit and value-group sequence and analyze the alternating determinant by valuation cases. -> Canonical residue character quotient preserving alternating pairs.
- **step-maximum-alternating-dimension** [claim](#stmt-local-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-dimension): Combine the local valuative-subspace theorem with the valuation transcendence-degree inequality and realize equality by a discrete flag. -> Intrinsic recovery of relative transcendence degree and defectless valuations at maximal dimension.
- **step-quasiprime-lines** [claim](#stmt-local-quasiprime) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-quasiprime): Use independent valuation approximation and coarsest associated valuations to recognize intersections of maximal alternating spaces. -> Unique quasi-prime valuation and decomposition space attached to each qualifying inertia line.
- **step-local-flag-correspondence** [claim](#stmt-local-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-transport): Recognize rationalized local spaces from the bracket, intersect with integral modules, and repeat on residue character quotients. -> Exact matched inertia and decomposition pairs along quasi-prime flags.
- **step-residual-criterion-hypotheses** [claim](#stmt-local-criterion-hypotheses) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-criterion-hypotheses): Use minimality to exclude divisible convex value subgroups and choose value-independent elements to form the auxiliary subfield. -> Both hypotheses needed for the residual-curve criterion.
- **step-finite-constant-curve-recognition** [claim](#stmt-local-curves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-curves): Transport the closure of the inertia union and apply the complete-curve-like residual criterion on both sides. -> Matched finite-field-closure constants and full residual point inertia families.
- **step-quasifinite-bertini** [claim](#stmt-tests-bertini) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tests-bertini): Analyze the square of the universal hyperplane incidence and compare the equal-image locus with its dominant complement. -> General geometrically irreducible hyperplane sections, with smoothness under the additional étale factorization.
- **step-finite-residual-testing** [claim](#stmt-tests-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tests-bertini-2): Spread the finite functions, divisors, root-cover components, and field presentation to a good finite residue fiber; cut by a general flag and compare relation kernels and transverse orders. -> Residual curve preserving all prescribed finite-level relations and support counts with model-dependent genus budget.
- **step-uniform-pencil-bound** [claim](#stmt-pencil-uniform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pencil-uniform): Test fixed anchors and an additional member at high finite levels, normalize the matched residual pencil, exclude the forbidden residue characteristic, compare genera, apply the different inequality, and pass through increasing supports. -> Uniform weighted exact-support bound for every member of the pencil.
- **step-individual-support-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-pencil-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pencil-finite): Apply the pencil bound to a nonconstant element and use divisibility of constants for a constant element. -> Finite support of every transformed ordinary multiplicative class.
- **step-infinite-divisor-family** [claim](#stmt-recovery-residue-powers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-recovery-residue-powers): Contrast the infinite pencil span with the finite-dimensional divisor-evaluation kernel, then use residual character evaluation outside finite supports. -> Infinite bounded-degree divisor family with pairwise cofinite residue-power tests.
- **step-fixed-incidence-curve** [claim](#stmt-recovery-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-recovery-incidence): Place divisors in bounded Hilbert families, fix an incidence parameter curve before testing, and extend each generic splitting over a normal finite base cover. -> A common finite field extension and regular curve field serving the entire test collection.
- **step-kummer-class-descent** [claim](#stmt-recovery-kummer-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-recovery-kummer-descent): Describe the ratios of a geometric root under a finite Galois constant extension and remove that character by Hilbert’s theorem. -> Unique descended base-field Kummer class.
- **step-finite-rank-curve-intersections** [claim](#stmt-recovery-intersection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-recovery-intersection): Prolong point valuations from the product of distinct curves and use vanishing on the other subfield to force all exact point orders to vanish. -> Finite rank of the intersection of distinct completed curve fields.
- **step-curve-correspondence-descent** [claim](#stmt-recovery-curves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-recovery-intersection-2): Apply the common incidence construction to all representatives, descend compatible Kummer classes, stabilize the curve field in a normal extension by infinite-rank intersection, then use norm and saturation before applying the inverse argument. -> Unique equality of matched completed curve fields in both directions.
- **step-divisorial-recognition** [claim](#stmt-recovery-divisorial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-recovery-divisorial): For a valuation trivial on constants choose a curve field embedded in the residue field; for nontrivial constant valuation exhibit a nonconstant principal unit in every curve field. -> Intrinsic distinction between true divisorial and other quasi-prime valuations.
- **step-rational-quotient-recovery** [claim](#stmt-recovery-rational) / [proof](#proof-stmt-recovery-rational): Saturate nonzero inertia restriction images, recover genus from point-order kernels, and dualize curve completion restrictions to obtain commuting quotient diagrams. -> Matched rational subfields with the actual restriction maps and point decomposition data.
- **step-pop-reconstruction-input** [claim](#stmt-reconstruction-pop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-reconstruction-pop): Invoke the cited functorial decomposition-graph result and the Isom reconstruction result with all geometric rational quotient diagrams. -> Perfect-closure isomorphism carrying constants onto constants, with the stated scalar and Frobenius ambiguities.
- **step-main-isom-bijection** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-reconstruction-pop-2): Lift the original isomorphism, recover its local and rational quotient data, verify graph-level compatibility by successive restriction and inertia quotient, then apply reconstruction and its uniqueness clause. -> Well-defined, surjective, and injective reconstruction map with only the prescribed global unit and Frobenius ambiguities.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-reconstruction-scope**: Bracket-compatible continuous module isomorphisms determine isomorphisms of perfect closures carrying the constant fields onto each other, with the prescribed scalar and Frobenius ambiguities.
- **invariant-uniform-pencil-support**: The support-degree bound is uniform over every member of a fixed pencil. Individual finite-level representatives do not determine the bound independently of the pencil.
- **invariant-residual-test-dependence**: A residual test curve may depend on the prescribed functions and exponent, while its genus bound depends on the fixed projective model.
- **invariant-curve-field-correspondence**: The completed multiplicative group of each curve subfield is carried onto that of a unique curve subfield on the other side.
- **invariant-saturated-point-inertia**: Point inertia is recovered by saturating nonzero images of divisorial inertia. The resulting correspondence preserves genus and identifies the rational subfields.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-outside-field-scope**: Apply the reconstruction statement to curve function fields, non-algebraically-closed constants, or the excluded characteristic. -> The proposed inputs do not satisfy the reconstruction theorem's hypotheses.
- **anti-abelian-input-alone**: Infer reconstruction from an arbitrary isomorphism of the abelian groups alone. -> An arbitrary abelian-group isomorphism need not preserve the commutator datum needed to recognize alternating pairs and valuations.
- **anti-residue-galois-identification**: Interpret all minimized residue character groups as ordinary Galois inertia and decomposition groups. -> The residue characteristic can equal the excluded prime during local recovery, so the ordinary Galois interpretation is not justified at that stage.
- **anti-fixed-residual-curve**: Reuse an arbitrary residual test curve for every collection of functions and every exponent. -> The finite-test construction permits the curve to vary with the finite data; only its genus is uniformly bounded.
- **anti-tame-hurwitz-substitution**: Replace the different inequality with the tame ramification formula in the pencil bound. -> Wild ramification is allowed, so the tame formula does not provide the stated justification.
- **anti-unsaturated-inertia-image**: Identify a nonzero restricted inertia image directly with the full point inertia line. -> The restricted image can have finite index, so it need not contain a generator of the full point line.
- **anti-independent-target-scalars**: Rescale each rational quotient target independently when applying the final reconstruction result. -> Independently adjusted targets do not establish the compatibility of the same fixed top-group isomorphism with all quotient diagrams.
- **anti-unquotiented-uniqueness**: Claim a unique original field isomorphism with no scalar or Frobenius ambiguity. -> The conclusion concerns perfect closures and explicitly permits the stated global scalar and Frobenius ambiguities.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The result reconstructs perfect closures from continuous pro-Galois data over arbitrary algebraically closed constants. Its geometric existence arguments are not a finite algorithm with a supplied finite input representation or executable output contract. The finite residual tests are existential constructions inside this proof, rather than a finite reconstruction procedure. The incidence argument explicitly allows an unrestricted collection: "The following geometric lemma permits an arbitrary collection of tests." It also requires an infinite divisor family: "The set $\mathcal S$ is infinite." A finite test harness would therefore check an auxiliary restriction and could not decide the reconstruction theorem.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The reconstruction input consists of continuous isomorphisms of completed Galois modules. The paper supplies no finite representation or executable procedure for obtaining those inputs or reconstructing the field. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The residual curves preserve algebraic power relations and divisor orders. Their role is a proof of field reconstruction; no evolving state, simulation rule or game mechanism is constructed. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The intrinsic inertia criterion requires alternating subspaces of the full rationalized character space. It gives a mathematical recognition condition without an effective method to enumerate candidates or decide the universal alternating relation. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The incidence lemma handles an arbitrary collection of power tests with common auxiliary fields. This simultaneous existence statement does not provide finite input encoding, termination or a procedure for selecting the fields. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The total decomposition graph records residue groups, subgroup inclusions and quotient maps along divisorial flags. The paper defines its mathematical contents without a finite storage layout or computational operations on a represented graph. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Continuous evaluation duality describes products of character modules and completed multiplicative groups. It does not specify finite arithmetic kernels, memory layouts or a hardware execution model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Kummer descent proves existence and uniqueness of power classes under regularity and roots-of-unity hypotheses. It supplies no cryptographic protocol, hardness assumption or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The correspondence between completed curve subfields is exact and quantified over every curve subfield. No training objective, statistical estimator or approximation guarantee converts that correspondence into a learning method. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniform pencil theorem bounds divisor-support degree. That mathematical bound is not an optimisation objective and comes without an algorithm for choosing a best model, pencil or residual test. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Commuting quotient diagrams express compatibility of field restriction with Galois-module isomorphisms. They do not specify distributed processes, message delivery or a consistency protocol. |
| other (Algebraic geometry and birational anabelian reconstruction) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The incidence proof provides a concrete mathematical construction: restrict the universal divisor family to a fixed parameter curve, take its function field and the parameter function field, and use the incidence projection to obtain a finite extension of the original function field. Adapting this construction requires supplying the geometric objects and proving the hypotheses; the paper does not give a finite executable selection procedure. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-reconstruction-field-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Reconstruction requires finitely generated function fields over algebraically closed constants, the excluded-characteristic condition, and the stated relative transcendence-degree threshold. Blocked use: ['Apply the main reconstruction theorem to curve function fields.', 'Apply the theorem over constants that are not algebraically closed.', 'Apply the theorem when a top field has characteristic equal to the fixed prime.']
- **limit-continuous-bracket-compatible-input** (hypothesis): An abelian module isomorphism alone does not meet the reconstruction input contract. Continuity and compatibility with the central commutator through a central-kernel isomorphism are required. Blocked use: ['Infer reconstruction from an arbitrary abstract abelian-group isomorphism.', 'Replace bracket compatibility by agreement on finitely many sampled pairs.']
- **limit-central-lift-selection** (nonconstructive): The central lift is obtained through projectivity and existence of a continuous section. The proof does not specify a finite procedure selecting that section or assert uniqueness of the lift. Blocked use: ['Treat the existence of a central lift as a canonical executable lifting operation.', 'Infer uniqueness of the central lift from uniqueness of the reconstructed field orbit.']
- **limit-residue-characteristic-interpretation** (hypothesis): Local recognition initially uses minimized character groups. Their identification with usual abelian Galois inertia and decomposition requires the characteristic restriction at both the field and its residue. Blocked use: ['Assign the usual Galois interpretation to every recovered residue group before checking its characteristic.', 'Exclude residue characteristic equal to the fixed prime merely from the top-field hypothesis.']
- **limit-quasiprime-line-recognition** (hypothesis): Recognizing quasi-prime inertia requires the stated dimension threshold and the intersection of maximal alternating subspaces. A line selected from an arbitrary character space is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Recognize a valuation from a candidate line without establishing the alternating-subspace intersection criterion.', 'Apply the line-recognition lemma below its relative transcendence-degree threshold.']
- **limit-residual-inertia-union** (formal_scope): The residual-curve criterion uses the closure of the union of quasi-prime inertia groups. Replacing that subset by the subgroup it generates changes the specified input. Blocked use: ['Compute the residual family from the closed subgroup generated by inertia rather than the closure of their union.']
- **limit-finite-constant-residual-recognition** (hypothesis): The complete-curve-like criterion identifies the full residual point inertia family under its verified hypotheses and finite-field-closure condition. This local result does not yet distinguish true divisors on the original field. Blocked use: ['Use residual point-family recognition as an immediate test for triviality of the original valuation on constants.', 'Claim the complete-curve-like point-family conclusion for arbitrary residual constants.']
- **limit-genus-comparison-characteristic** (hypothesis): Genus comparison through Picard torsion and point-order kernels must wait until residue characteristic different from the fixed prime has been established. Blocked use: ['Recover genus by the prime-to-characteristic Picard-torsion formula in residue characteristic equal to the fixed prime.']
- **limit-residual-tests-vary-with-data** (formal_scope): Finite residual tests preserve the requested finite collection at its chosen exponent. The curve may vary with those data; uniformity concerns the genus bound on a fixed projective model. Blocked use: ['Reuse an arbitrary residual curve as a universal witness for all functions and exponents.', 'Infer preservation of untested Kummer relations from a residual test for a specified finite collection.']
- **limit-bertini-map-and-generality** (hypothesis): The hyperplane lemma requires an integral variety over algebraically closed constants, a quasi-finite map, and the stated dimension threshold. Its smoothness conclusion additionally requires the specified finite étale factorization, and its conclusions concern general hyperplanes. Blocked use: ['Assert irreducibility for every chosen hyperplane.', 'Drop quasi-finiteness or the source dimension condition.', 'Infer smoothness from the irreducibility conclusion without checking the additional factorization.']
- **limit-pencil-anchor-normalization** (hypothesis): The pencil proof fixes distinct anchors and imposes additional anchor choices in characteristic zero to exclude the forbidden residue characteristic before comparing genera. Blocked use: ["Remove the characteristic-sensitive anchor choices while retaining the proof's residue-characteristic conclusion.", 'Replace the distinct anchor fibers by repeated values in the ramification estimate.']
- **limit-separable-hurwitz-with-wild-ramification** (hypothesis): The ramification estimate concerns the separable residual map after inseparability is removed. Wild ramification is allowed, so the proof needs the different inequality. Blocked use: ['Apply the separable degree argument directly to the residual function before removing inseparability.', 'Substitute a tame-only ramification formula for the different inequality.']
- **limit-finite-level-support-threshold** (formal_scope): The finite-level support estimate is established at every sufficiently high exponent for each fixed pencil member. The proof permits the necessary exponent threshold to depend on that member and derives exact support by an increasing-union argument. Blocked use: ['Claim that the proof supplies a common finite testing threshold for all pencil members.', 'Treat a single finite-level support as a complete enumeration of exact support.']
- **limit-pencil-bound-parameter-scope** (formal_scope): The support bound is uniform in the member of a fixed pencil, with the model, isomorphism, parameter, and anchors fixed. The proof does not assert a common bound over all models or all pencils. Blocked use: ['Use the fixed-pencil bound as a universal bound for every nonconstant parameter.', 'Carry the same numerical support budget across changes of the projective model without justification.']
- **limit-incidence-hypotheses-and-exceptions** (hypothesis): Incidence recovery requires an infinite bounded-degree family of prime divisors on a normal integral projective variety. Each prime-to-characteristic power test has its own finite exceptional set; a common exceptional set is not assumed. Blocked use: ['Apply incidence recovery to a finite family or a family without bounded degree.', 'Apply the power-test argument to an exponent divisible by the characteristic.', 'Require or infer a common cofinite subset satisfying every test simultaneously.']
- **limit-fixed-incidence-curve-base-change** (formal_scope): The incidence parameter curve is fixed before individual tests. Generic splitting for a test need not hold on a dense open meeting that curve; the normal finite base-change argument is needed to extend splitting to it. Blocked use: ['Choose a different parameter curve for each test while claiming common auxiliary fields.', 'Infer splitting on the fixed curve solely from splitting on a dense open of the larger parameter space.']
- **limit-kummer-descent-prerequisites** (hypothesis): Unique descent of a geometrically split power class requires a regular extension, an exponent prime to the characteristic, and the required roots of unity in the base. Blocked use: ['Use the unique descended-class conclusion without proving regularity.', 'Omit the roots-of-unity inclusion or the prime-to-characteristic condition.']
- **limit-curve-completion-and-norm-descent** (hypothesis): Curve subfields must contain the constants and be relatively algebraically closed. Finite-rank intersections require distinct curve subfields, while norm descent removes the extension-degree multiplier using saturation of the descended completion. Blocked use: ['Apply the curve-field correspondence to an arbitrary subfield of transcendence degree one.', 'Replace finite-rank intersection by a claim that distinct completed curve fields have trivial intersection.', 'Cancel the extension-degree multiplier without proving the required saturation.']
- **limit-true-divisorial-recognition** (formal_scope): Recovered quasi-prime divisors can be nontrivial on constants. True divisorial recognition needs the additional completed curve-field test, so the local correspondence alone is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Identify every quasi-prime divisor with a geometric divisorial valuation before recovering curve subfields.']
- **limit-point-inertia-saturation-and-map-retention** (formal_scope): Nonzero restricted inertia images may have finite index. Saturation identifies the full point inertia line, but graph compatibility must retain the actual subgroup images and restriction maps. Blocked use: ['Identify an unsaturated nonzero image with the entire point inertia line.', 'Discard actual subgroup images and maps after recognizing their saturated lines.']
- **limit-global-rational-quotient-compatibility** (hypothesis): Final field reconstruction requires compatibility with all geometric rational quotient diagrams, including their point data, for the fixed top-group map and its induced target maps. Blocked use: ['Apply final reconstruction from a bare decomposition graph without rational quotient compatibility.', 'Rescale quotient targets independently to obtain separately commuting diagrams.', 'Replace graph-level compatibility with agreement on an arbitrary finite selection of quotients.']
- **limit-perfect-closure-and-ambiguity** (formal_scope): The reconstructed field is the perfect closure. The induced top-group map is specified up to a global unit, and positive-characteristic field uniqueness is modulo Frobenius twists. Blocked use: ['Claim reconstruction of a unique original imperfect field.', 'Claim an unquotiented unique field isomorphism in positive characteristic.', 'Treat the Galois-side scalar ambiguity as separately adjustable on each quotient.']
- **limit-no-finite-reconstruction-certificate** (nonconstructive): The reconstruction proof uses infinite divisor families, arbitrary collections of tests, inverse limits, and continuous group data. It supplies no finite reconstruction input encoding, executable reconstruction procedure, or computational complexity guarantee. Blocked use: ['Present finite enumeration of auxiliary examples as a certificate of the full reconstruction theorem.', 'Assign running-time or memory bounds to a reconstruction algorithm not supplied by the source.', 'Interpret existential finite residual tests as a finite decision procedure for the global theorem.']

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The Bogomolov-Pop reconstruction theorem](../../preprints/The-Bogomolov-Pop-reconstruction-theorem-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The Bogomolov–Pop reconstruction conjecture concerns recovering a function field and its constants from pro-$\ell$ abelian-by-central Galois data. The linked formalization proves injectivity of the reconstruction correspondence for function fields of transcendence degree at least two over algebraically closed fields of characteristic different from the prime $\ell$.
>
> If two isomorphisms of perfect closures preserving the constant fields induce the same Galois-data isomorphism modulo $\ell$-adic unit scaling, then they agree modulo Frobenius. The selected statement proves this uniqueness; existence of a field isomorphism for every admissible Galois-data isomorphism is outside it.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
namespace BogomolovPop

def proEllKernel (ell : ℕ) (G : Type*) [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] : Subgroup G :=
  ⨅ (H : OpenNormalSubgroup G) (_ : IsPGroup ell (G ⧸ H.toSubgroup)), H.toSubgroup

instance proEllKernel_normal (ell : ℕ) (G : Type*) [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] :
    (proEllKernel ell G).Normal := by
  unfold proEllKernel
  exact Subgroup.normal_iInf_normal fun H =>
    Subgroup.normal_iInf_normal fun _ => H.isNormal'

abbrev ProEll (ell : ℕ) (F : Type*) [Field F] :=
  Field.absoluteGaloisGroup F ⧸ proEllKernel ell (Field.absoluteGaloisGroup F)

def closedGammaThree (G : Type*) [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] [IsTopologicalGroup G] :
    Subgroup G :=
  (⁅(commutator G).topologicalClosure, (⊤ : Subgroup G)⁆).topologicalClosure

instance closedGammaThree_normal (G : Type*) [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G]
    [IsTopologicalGroup G] : (closedGammaThree G).Normal := by
  unfold closedGammaThree
  exact Subgroup.is_normal_topologicalClosure _

abbrev PiA (ell : ℕ) (F : Type*) [Field F] := TopologicalAbelianization (ProEll ell F)

abbrev PiC (ell : ℕ) (F : Type*) [Field F] := ProEll ell F ⧸ closedGammaThree (ProEll ell F)

theorem closedGammaThree_le (G : Type*) [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G]
    [IsTopologicalGroup G] : closedGammaThree G ≤ (commutator G).topologicalClosure := by
  exact Subgroup.topologicalClosure_minimal _ (Subgroup.commutator_le_left _ _)
    (Subgroup.isClosed_topologicalClosure _)

def centralProjection (ell : ℕ) (F : Type*) [Field F] : PiC ell F →* PiA ell F :=
  QuotientGroup.map _ _ (MonoidHom.id _) (closedGammaThree_le _)

abbrev Delta (ell : ℕ) (F : Type*) [Field F] := (centralProjection ell F).ker

def sourceCommutator {G : Type*} [Group G] (x y : G) : G := x⁻¹ * y⁻¹ * x * y

def abelianLift (ell : ℕ) (F : Type*) [Field F] (x : PiA ell F) : ProEll ell F :=
  Quotient.out x

theorem abelianLift_spec (ell : ℕ) (F : Type*) [Field F] (x : PiA ell F) :
    QuotientGroup.mk' _ (abelianLift ell F x) = x := Quotient.out_eq x

def bracket (ell : ℕ) (F : Type*) [Field F] (x y : PiA ell F) : Delta ell F :=
  ⟨QuotientGroup.mk' _ (sourceCommutator (abelianLift ell F x) (abelianLift ell F y)), by
    change centralProjection ell F _ = 1
    simp only [centralProjection, sourceCommutator, map_mul, map_inv]
    simp [mul_comm]⟩

def BracketCompatible {ell : ℕ} {K L : Type*} [Field K] [Field L]
    (φ : PiA ell L ≃ₜ* PiA ell K) : Prop :=
  ∃ ψ : Delta ell L ≃ₜ* Delta ell K,
    ∀ σ τ, ψ (bracket ell L σ τ) = bracket ell K (φ σ) (φ τ)

abbrev PerfectFieldClosure (F : Type*) [Field F] := perfectClosure F (AlgebraicClosure F)

def PerfectIsom (k K l L : Type*) [Field k] [Field K] [Field l] [Field L]
    [Algebra k K] [Algebra l L] :=
  { α : PerfectFieldClosure K ≃+* PerfectFieldClosure L //
    α '' Set.range (algebraMap k (PerfectFieldClosure K)) =
      Set.range (algebraMap l (PerfectFieldClosure L)) }

def FrobeniusStep {k K l L : Type*} [Field k] [Field K] [Field l] [Field L]
    [Algebra k K] [Algebra l L] (α β : PerfectIsom k K l L) : Prop :=
  ∃ p : ℕ, p.Prime ∧ (p : L) = 0 ∧
    ∀ x : PerfectFieldClosure K, β.1 x = (α.1 x) ^ p

abbrev IsomF (k K l L : Type*) [Field k] [Field K] [Field l] [Field L]
    [Algebra k K] [Algebra l L] :=
  Quotient (Relation.EqvGen.setoid (FrobeniusStep (k := k) (K := K) (l := l) (L := L)))

abbrev IsomC (ell : ℕ) (K L : Type*) [Field K] [Field L] :=
  {φ : PiA ell L ≃ₜ* PiA ell K // BracketCompatible φ}

def ScalarStep {ell : ℕ} [Fact ell.Prime] {K L : Type*} [Field K] [Field L]
    (φ χ : IsomC ell K L) : Prop :=
  ∃ u : (PadicInt ell)ˣ, ∀ x : PiA ell L,
    Filter.Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => (φ.1 x) ^ PadicInt.appr (u : PadicInt ell) n)
      Filter.atTop (nhds (χ.1 x))

abbrev IsomCUnits (ell : ℕ) [Fact ell.Prime] (K L : Type*) [Field K] [Field L] :=
  Quotient (Relation.EqvGen.setoid (ScalarStep (ell := ell) (K := K) (L := L)))

def absoluteToPiA (ell : ℕ) (F : Type*) [Field F] :
    Field.absoluteGaloisGroup F →* PiA ell F :=
  (QuotientGroup.mk' _).comp (QuotientGroup.mk' _)

def absoluteAction {F : Type*} [Field F] (σ : Field.absoluteGaloisGroup F)
    (x : AlgebraicClosure F) : AlgebraicClosure F := by
  change AlgebraicClosure F ≃ₐ[F] AlgebraicClosure F at σ
  exact σ x

def PullbackGraph {ell : ℕ} {k K l L : Type*}
    [Field k] [Field K] [Field l] [Field L] [Algebra k K] [Algebra l L]
    (α : PerfectIsom k K l L) (φ : IsomC ell K L) : Prop :=
  ∃ β : AlgebraicClosure K ≃+* AlgebraicClosure L,
    (∀ x : PerfectFieldClosure K, β x = ((α.1 x : PerfectFieldClosure L) :
      AlgebraicClosure L)) ∧
    ∀ σ : Field.absoluteGaloisGroup L, ∃ τ : Field.absoluteGaloisGroup K,
      (∀ x : AlgebraicClosure K,
        β (absoluteAction τ x) = absoluteAction σ (β x)) ∧
      φ.1 (absoluteToPiA ell L σ) = absoluteToPiA ell K τ

def PhiGraph {ell : ℕ} [Fact ell.Prime] {k K l L : Type*}
    [Field k] [Field K] [Field l] [Field L] [Algebra k K] [Algebra l L]
    (a : IsomF k K l L) (b : IsomCUnits ell K L) : Prop :=
  ∃ α : PerfectIsom k K l L, ∃ φ : IsomC ell K L,
    Quotient.mk _ α = a ∧ Quotient.mk _ φ = b ∧ PullbackGraph α φ

theorem main_graph_injective (ell : ℕ) [Fact ell.Prime]
    (k K l L : Type*) [Field k] [Field K] [Field l] [Field L]
    [IsAlgClosed k] [IsAlgClosed l] [Algebra k K] [Algebra l L]
    [Algebra.EssFiniteType k K] [Algebra.EssFiniteType l L]
    (hk : (ell : k) ≠ 0) (hl : (ell : l) ≠ 0)
    (hK : 2 ≤ Algebra.trdeg k K) (hL : 2 ≤ Algebra.trdeg l L) :
    ∀ (a a' : IsomF k K l L) (b : IsomCUnits ell K L),
      PhiGraph a b → PhiGraph a' b → a = a'  := by
  sorry

end BogomolovPop

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Bogomolov–Pop reconstruction conjecture for function fields of transcendence degree at least two over arbitrary algebraically closed fields of characteristic different from <i>ℓ</i>. The pro-<i>ℓ</i> abelian-by-central datum determines the perfect closure and its constant field, with precisely the Frobenius and <i>ℓ</i>-adic unit ambiguities.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

|---|---|---|---|---|

| [stmt-prelim-duality](#stmt-prelim-duality) | lemma | `prelim:duality` | 2 | The group $F^\times/\kappa^\times$ is free abelian. |
| [stmt-prelim-completion](#stmt-prelim-completion) | lemma | `prelim:completion` | 3 | Let $E\subset F$ be function fields over the same algebraically closed field $\kappa$.* |
| [stmt-prelim-central-lift](#stmt-prelim-central-lift) | lemma | `prelim:central-lift` | 4 | Every bracket-compatible isomorphism in Theorem 1 is the abelianization of a continuous isomorphism $\Pi_L^c\to\Pi_K^c$. |
| [stmt-prelim-curve-kernel](#stmt-prelim-curve-kernel) | lemma | `prelim:curve-kernel` | 5 | If $\operatorname{char}\kappa\ne\ell$, then |
| [stmt-local-commuting](#stmt-local-commuting) | lemma | `local:commuting` | 6 | Let $F/\kappa$ be a function field over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $\ell$. |
| [stmt-local-residue](#stmt-local-residue) | lemma | `local:residue` | 7 | For a quasi-prime $r$-divisor $v$ of $F/\kappa$, restriction to units induces a canonical topological isomorphism $$D_v/I_v\cong W_{Fv}.$$ If $f,g\in\mathcal D_v$, they are alternating if and only if |
| [stmt-local-dimension](#stmt-local-dimension) | lemma | `local:dimension` | 8 | For $F/\kappa$ as above, in any characteristic, $s$ is the maximum dimension of an alternating subspace of $\mathcal G_F$. |
| [stmt-local-quasiprime](#stmt-local-quasiprime) | lemma | `local:quasiprime` | 9 | Assume $s\geq2$. A line $H\subset\mathcal G_F$ is the inertia space of a quasi-prime divisor if and only if there are two $s$-dimensional alternating subspaces $S_1,S_2$ with $S_1\cap S_2=H$. |
| [stmt-local-transport](#stmt-local-transport) | proposition | `local:transport` | 10 | Let $K/k$ and $L/l$ be function fields over algebraically closed fields of characteristic different from $\ell$, each of transcendence degree at least two. |
| [stmt-local-criterion-hypotheses](#stmt-local-criterion-hypotheses) | lemma | `local:criterion-hypotheses` | 11 | Every quasi-prime $(s-1)$-divisor $v$ of $F/\kappa$ satisfies the two hypotheses of the residual-curve criterion.* |
| [stmt-local-curves](#stmt-local-curves) | proposition | `local:curves` | 12 | In the setting of Proposition 10, let $w$ and $v$ be matched quasi-prime $(d-1)$-divisors. |

| [stmt-pencil-finite](#stmt-pencil-finite) | corollary | `pencil:finite` | 16 | For every $z\in K^\times$, the class $\Theta[z]$ has finite support on $X$.* |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### The reconstruction problem

Birational anabelian geometry asks how much of a field can be read from its Galois groups. Over algebraically closed constants, the arithmetic Galois action disappears. Bogomolov’s program proposes that a particularly small quotient still remembers every function field of dimension at least two: the quotient in which commutators are central. We prove the Isom form of this assertion, including surfaces and the prime $2$.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The exact statement

Fix a prime $\ell$. A function field $F/\kappa$ in this paper is a finitely generated extension of an algebraically closed field $\kappa$. For $\operatorname{char}F\ne\ell$, let $G_F^{(\ell)}$ be the maximal pro-$\ell$ quotient of the absolute Galois group, and set $$\Pi_F^a=G_F^{(\ell)}/[G_F^{(\ell)},G_F^{(\ell)}],\qquad
 \Pi_F^c=G_F^{(\ell)}/[[G_F^{(\ell)},G_F^{(\ell)}],G_F^{(\ell)}].$$ All commutator subgroups are closed. The kernel $\Delta_F=\ker(\Pi_F^c\to\Pi_F^a)$ is central. Our commutator convention is $[x,y]=x^{-1}y^{-1}xy$. Lifting elements of $\Pi_F^a$ to $\Pi_F^c$ therefore defines a continuous alternating $\mathbb Z_\ell$-bilinear map $$[\ ,\ ]_F:\Pi_F^a\times\Pi_F^a\longrightarrow\Delta_F.$$

An isomorphism $\varphi:\Pi_L^a\to\Pi_K^a$ is *bracket-compatible* if it is a continuous $\mathbb Z_\ell$-module isomorphism and there is a continuous $\mathbb Z_\ell$-module isomorphism $\psi:\Delta_L\to\Delta_K$ such that $$\psi([\sigma,\tau]_L)=[\varphi\sigma,\varphi\tau]_K
 \quad(\sigma,\tau\in\Pi_L^a).$$ Write $\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}^c(\Pi_L^a,\Pi_K^a)$ for this set. The units $\mathbb Z_\ell^\times$ act by multiplication on $\varphi$; the corresponding map on the central kernel can be multiplied by the square of the same unit.

Write $F^i$ for the perfect closure of $F$; in characteristic zero this is $F$ itself. Let $\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}^i(K,L)$ consist of the field isomorphisms $\alpha:K^i\to L^i$ satisfying $\alpha(k)=l$. If the common characteristic is $p>0$, put $$\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}_F^i(K,L)=\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}^i(K,L)/
 \bigl(\alpha\sim\mathop{\mathrm{Frob}}_L^n\circ\alpha,\ n\in\mathbb Z\bigr).$$ Frobenius is an automorphism of a perfect field, so negative integers are allowed. In characteristic zero take no quotient. If the characteristics differ, set $\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}_F^i(K,L)=\varnothing$.

Field isomorphisms induce contravariant Galois isomorphisms. Passing to the above quotients gives the canonical map in the following theorem.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Bogomolov–Pop reconstruction). *Let $k$ and $l$ be arbitrary algebraically closed fields of characteristics different from $\ell$, and let $K/k$ and $L/l$ be function fields with $\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(K/k),\mathop{\mathrm{trdeg}}(L/l)\ge2$. Then $$\Phi_{K,L}:\mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}_F^i(K,L)\longrightarrow
 \mathop{\mathrm{Isom}}^c(\Pi_L^a,\Pi_K^a)/\mathbb Z_\ell^\times,
 \qquad [\alpha]\longmapsto[\alpha^*],$$ is a bijection.*

</div>

Thus the theorem resolves the pro-$\ell$ abelian-by-central Bogomolov–Pop reconstruction conjecture positively. Neither equality of characteristics nor equality of relative transcendence degrees is assumed. Both are forced by a bracket-compatible isomorphism. The input contains no distinguished valuations, inertia groups, or curve quotients.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Previous work and the proof

Bogomolov introduced the abelian-by-central approach to birational reconstruction (F. A. Bogomolov 1991) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Bogomolov1991](#ref-Bogomolov1991)]. The local approach detects valuations through commuting elements and their associated multiplicative characters (F. Bogomolov and Tschinkel 2002; Topaz 2017) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BT2002](#ref-BT2002), [TopazCL2017](#ref-TopazCL2017)]. Bogomolov and Tschinkel proved the surface case over algebraic closures of finite fields (F. Bogomolov and Tschinkel 2008) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BT2008](#ref-BT2008)], followed by higher-dimensional reconstruction (F. Bogomolov and Tschinkel 2011) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BT2011](#ref-BT2011)]; Pop established a corresponding birational reconstruction theorem (Pop 2012a) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[PopBI2012](#ref-PopBI2012)]. The precise Isom formulation, including its scalar and Frobenius ambiguities, is recorded in (Topaz 2016, Introduction, Conjecture 1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[TopazRat2016](#ref-TopazRat2016)].

Pop’s global reconstruction theorem recovers a function field from its total decomposition graph together with suitable rational quotients (Pop 2012b, 2012a) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[PopGraphs2012](#ref-PopGraphs2012), [PopBI2012](#ref-PopBI2012)]. His later reconstruction theorem with divisorial inertia applies in relative transcendence degree greater than two (Pop 2018, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Pop2018](#ref-Pop2018)]; its additional inertia datum must itself be recovered to solve the conjecture from the group alone. The local theory of commuting pairs supplies quasi-divisorial valuations, which can be nontrivial on the constants (Pop 2018; Topaz 2019) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Pop2018](#ref-Pop2018), [Topaz2019](#ref-Topaz2019)]. Pop’s minimized decomposition theory also recognizes certain residual curves over algebraic closures of finite fields, with Topaz’s appendix treating the required residue characteristic $\ell$ case (Pop 2015) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[PopMin2015](#ref-PopMin2015)].

Related reconstruction results use additional structure. Silberstein studies the full absolute Galois group under a prime-field-closure hypothesis (Silberstein 2013) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Silberstein2013](#ref-Silberstein2013)]. Topaz’s integral cohomological reconstruction uses a specified arithmetic Galois action in transcendence degree at least three (Topaz 2019, Theorems A and B) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Topaz2019](#ref-Topaz2019)]; his Torelli-type theorem uses mixed Hodge structures and cup products over constants embedded in $\mathbb C$ (Topaz 2025) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[TopazTorelli2025](#ref-TopazTorelli2025)]. These results concern different input data. Here only the original pro-$\ell$ abelian-by-central datum is supplied, and neither the constant field nor geometric inertia is distinguished in advance.

Our task is to recover the missing geometric information uniformly over the constants. Write $\widehat F=\varprojlim_e F^\times/F^{\times\ell^e}$ for the completed multiplicative group and $[f]$ for the class of a nonzero function. Choose Kummer identifications, and dualize a bracket-compatible isomorphism to an isomorphism $\Theta:\widehat K\to\widehat L$ of completed multiplicative groups. The central step proves that, for fixed $t\in K\setminus k$, the entire pencil $$h_a=\Theta[t-a],\qquad a\in k,$$ has uniformly bounded divisor-support degree on a fixed projective model of $L$. This is stronger than finiteness of each individual support.

Bogomolov–Tschinkel already used bounded-genus curves and Hurwitz estimates to prove finiteness of $\ell$-adic supports (F. Bogomolov and Tschinkel 2011, sec. 6, proof of Proposition 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[BT2011](#ref-BT2011)]. Here the bound is uniform over a whole pencil and is obtained by finite tests over arbitrary constants. A finite list of Kummer classes is specialized and restricted to a smooth residual curve of bounded genus, preserving all relations at the chosen finite level and the relevant divisor supports. Five fixed members of the pencil then bound the degree of a separable map to the projective line by Riemann–Hurwitz. The bound also controls every additional member, at every sufficiently high $\ell$-power level. The use of five anchors works for $\ell=2$.

The pencil detects infinitely many prime divisors, and the uniform degree bound places them in a family of finite type. Their incidence with the model produces a curve subfield after a finite field extension. A normal-base-change argument allows us to use one parameter curve for every Kummer class and every exponent; no uncountability assumption enters. Norms and the finite-rank intersection of distinct completed curve fields descend this subfield to $L$. Applying the same argument to $\Theta^{-1}$ gives a bijection of the completed relatively algebraically closed curve subfields.

These subfields distinguish true divisorial valuations from those that also value the constants. They also recover point inertia and genus, and hence all relatively algebraically closed rational subfields. Their quotient diagrams supply exactly the input to Pop’s global reconstruction theorem. The new geometric steps are the uniform pencil bound and the incidence argument that recovers curve subfields; the remaining steps use the local valuation theory and Pop’s reconstruction theorem described above.

The proof follows this order. Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> fixes the Kummer topology and the relation between the two formulations of the group input. Section <a href="#local:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="local:section">3</a> supplies the local correspondence. Sections <a href="#sec:tests" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tests">[sec:tests]</a> and <a href="#sec:pencil" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pencil">5</a> prove the residual test and uniform support bound. Section <a href="#sec:curve-recovery" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:curve-recovery">6</a> recovers curve subfields and rational quotients. Section <a href="#sec:reconstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reconstruction">7</a> checks compatibility on the decomposition graphs before applying Pop’s theorem and completing the Isom statement and its ambiguity checks.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Kummer duality and the group input

We first give the topological form of Kummer duality needed for selected subfields and residue fields. In this section the residue characteristic is allowed to equal $\ell$ unless explicitly excluded.

For a function field $F/\kappa$ over algebraically closed constants, put $$W_F=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(F^\times,\mathbb Z_\ell),\qquad
 \widehat F=\varprojlim_{e\ge1} F^\times/F^{\times\ell^e}.$$ Equip $W_F$ with pointwise convergence and $\widehat F$ with its $\ell$-adic topology. We use additive notation for these modules and write $[f]$ for the class of $f\in F^\times$. In particular $[fg]=[f]+[g]$. All characters vanish on $\kappa^\times$. We abbreviate $F^\times/F^{\times q}$ to $F^\times/q$. An annihilator $B^\perp$ of $B\subset F^\times$ or $B\subset\widehat F$ is taken in $W_F$; for $B\subset W_F$ it is taken in $\widehat F$, using evaluation.

<a id="stmt-prelim-duality"></a>
<div id="prelim:duality" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Continuous evaluation). *The group $F^\times/\kappa^\times$ is free abelian. Consequently $W_F$ is a product of copies of $\mathbb Z_\ell$, and evaluation gives $$\widehat F=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{cont}}(W_F,\mathbb Z_\ell),\qquad
 \widehat F/\ell^e\widehat F=F^\times/F^{\times\ell^e}.$$ The module $\widehat F$ is torsion free, and evaluations on $W_F$ detect every finite-level class.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prelim-duality"><summary>Proof of stmt-prelim-duality</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a normal integral projective model of $F/\kappa$. A rational function with zero Weil divisor on this model is a global unit and hence a constant. Thus the divisor map embeds $F^\times/\kappa^\times$ in the free abelian group of prime divisors; a subgroup of a free abelian group is free.

For a basis indexed by a set $J$, the character group is $\mathbb Z_\ell^J$. Modulo $\ell^e$, a continuous character on this compact product depends on finitely many coordinates. Compatible such characters are therefore families $(c_j)_{j\in J}$ in $\mathbb Z_\ell$ with only finitely many $c_j\notin\ell^e\mathbb Z_\ell$ for each $e$. This is exactly the completion of $\bigoplus_{j\in J}\mathbb Z$. Reduction modulo $\ell^e$ and coordinate evaluations prove the assertions. Divisibility of $\kappa^\times$ removes the constants at every level. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:theta"></a>
When $\operatorname{char}F\ne\ell$, a choice of a compatible system of $\ell$-power roots of unity identifies the Tate module $\mathbb Z_\ell(1)$ with $\mathbb Z_\ell$. Kummer theory then identifies $\Pi_F^a$ with $W_F$. An isomorphism $\varphi:\Pi_L^a\to\Pi_K^a$ has evaluation dual $$\label{eq:theta}
 \Theta:\widehat K\longrightarrow\widehat L,
 \qquad \Theta(h)(\sigma)=h(\varphi\sigma).$$ Changing the Tate identifications multiplies this map by one unit. We fix the identifications for the proof.

<a id="stmt-prelim-completion"></a>
<div id="prelim:completion" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Subfields and finite extensions). *Let $E\subset F$ be function fields over the same algebraically closed field $\kappa$.*

1.  *If $E$ is relatively algebraically closed in $F$, then $\widehat E\to\widehat F$ is injective, its image is closed and $\ell$-saturated, and restriction $W_F\to W_E$ is a continuous surjection. Here $\ell$-saturated means that $\ell h\in\widehat E$ for $h\in\widehat F$ implies $h\in\widehat E$.*

2.  *If $F/E$ is finite, its completion map is injective, and inclusion followed by norm on completions is multiplication by $[F:E]$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prelim-completion"><summary>Proof of stmt-prelim-completion</summary>

*Proof.* In the first case, a root in $F$ of an element of $E$ is algebraic over $E$, so injectivity holds at every finite level. Alternatively, on a normal integral projective $E$-model of $F$, the divisor map embeds $F^\times/E^\times$ into a free abelian group: its kernel consists of global units, which are algebraic over $E$ and therefore lie in $E$. Thus $F^\times/E^\times$ is free abelian, and $$0\longrightarrow E^\times/\kappa^\times
 \longrightarrow F^\times/\kappa^\times
 \longrightarrow F^\times/E^\times\longrightarrow0$$ splits as a sequence of abelian groups. Completion preserves this splitting. The two completed free factors are torsion free, proving closedness and saturation. Every character on $E^\times$ extends along the splitting, proving the asserted surjection; continuity follows from the pointwise topologies.

For a finite extension, the field norm is defined also in the inseparable case and sends an element of $E$ to its $[F:E]$-th power. It induces the stated composite on completions. Torsion freeness from Lemma <a href="#prelim:duality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:duality">2</a> proves injectivity. ◻

</details>

The next observation lets us apply Pop’s reconstruction theorems to the stated group input.

<a id="stmt-prelim-central-lift"></a>
<div id="prelim:central-lift" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Lifting bracket-compatible isomorphisms). *Every bracket-compatible isomorphism in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is the abelianization of a continuous isomorphism $\Pi_L^c\to\Pi_K^c$. This holds also for $\ell=2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prelim-central-lift"><summary>Proof of stmt-prelim-central-lift</summary>

*Proof.* Use the given maps to identify the bases of the two central extensions with one abelian pro-$\ell$ group $A$ and their kernels with one group $B$. Let $E_1,E_2$ denote these extensions. In the fiber product $P=E_1\times_A E_2$, quotient by the diagonal central subgroup $\{(b,b):b\in B\}$. The resulting group $Q$ is abelian: the commutator of two pairs has identical components by bracket compatibility. It fits into an exact sequence of abelian pro-$\ell$ groups $$0\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow0.$$ By Lemma <a href="#prelim:duality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:duality">2</a>, $A$ is a product of copies of $\mathbb Z_\ell$. Such products are projective in the category of abelian pro-$\ell$ groups. Indeed Pontryagin duality changes $A$ into a direct sum of copies of $\mathbb Q_\ell/\mathbb Z_\ell$, a divisible, hence injective, discrete torsion group. Consequently $Q\to A$ has a continuous section.

The inverse image of this section in $P$ maps isomorphically to each $E_i$. For fixed first component, the second component can be adjusted by a unique element of $B$ to land over the section; its uniqueness also proves that each projection has trivial kernel. These are continuous bijections between compact Hausdorff groups, hence isomorphisms. The resulting map $E_1\to E_2$ induces the prescribed base and kernel maps. The proof uses no division by $2$ and leaves no additional power datum to specify. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Orders on a curve

For a smooth projective integral curve $C/\kappa$ with function field $E$, its closed points are $\kappa$-rational. Each normalized order $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_P:E^\times\to\mathbb Z$ extends to $\widehat E\to\mathbb Z_\ell$.

<a id="stmt-prelim-curve-kernel"></a>
<div id="prelim:curve-kernel" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Curve kernels). *If $\operatorname{char}\kappa\ne\ell$, then $$\ker\left(E^\times/E^{\times\ell}
       \xrightarrow{(\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_P)_P}\bigoplus_{P\in C}\mathbb Z/\ell\right)
 \simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(C)[\ell].$$ Its dimension over $\mathbb F_\ell$ is $2g(C)$. The kernel of all exact point orders on $\widehat E$ is the Tate module $T_\ell\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(C)$ and has $\mathbb Z_\ell$-rank $2g(C)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prelim-curve-kernel"><summary>Proof of stmt-prelim-curve-kernel</summary>

*Proof.* The same argument works with $q=\ell^e$. If $\operatorname{div}(f)=qD$, send $[f]$ to the divisor class of $D$. Changing $f$ by a $q$-th power does not change this class. Every $q$-torsion divisor class occurs. The kernel consists of functions $cu^q$; the constant $c\in\kappa^\times$ is a $q$-th power. This proves the finite-level identification. The $q$-torsion of the Jacobian over an algebraically closed field of characteristic prime to $q$ is $(\mathbb Z/q)^{2g(C)}$ (The Stacks Project Authors 2026, Tag 0C1Z, Lemma 53.17.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Stacks](#ref-Stacks)]. Under reduction from $\ell^{e+1}$ to $\ell^e$, the divisor class is multiplied by $\ell$. Taking the inverse limit gives the exact-order kernel and its rank. ◻

</details>

In particular, an isomorphism of the $W$-groups carrying the family of point inertia lines bijectively onto the corresponding family preserves the genus.
